# Findings library: things in this codebase worth a second look

Review run 2026-11-19 to 2026-11-20. Nothing in this file has been fixed unless an entry says so.

## How this was made

- 33 reviewers each read one area of the code (read-only) and reported defects with a file, line, mechanism and trigger.
- Reports describing the same defect were merged: 269 raw reports became 166 distinct findings.
- Every finding was then checked twice by independent checkers told to try to disprove it: one checked that the code really does what the report says, the other that it can actually happen on this robot and whether the fix is safe.
- No robot was available. Nothing here was tested on hardware.

## Totals

| | Count |
|---|---|
| Confirmed (neither check disproved it) | 164 |
| Disputed (one check disproved it) | 1 |
| Disproved by both checks and dropped | 1 |
| Severity of the 165 kept: high / medium / low | 2 / 17 / 146 |

## How to read an entry

- **ID**: area prefix and a number; lower numbers are more severe within the area.
- **Severity** is the lower of the two checkers' ratings. Where the reviewer rated it higher, that is shown.
- **Needs robot**: whether a fix can be made safely with no robot to test on. The more cautious of the two checkers' answers is used.
- **Changes interface**: the fix would change how `moveToPoint`, `moveToPose`, the turns and so on are called, or how autons and driver code are normally written. Those are parked for the team to decide.
- **Already known**: overlaps something found before this review (see `docs/HANDOFF.md`).
- Line numbers are as of the review. `src/lemlib/chassis/motions/moveToPose.cpp` gained 5 lines after line 115 during the review.

## 1. Safe to fix without a robot (149)

Confirmed, the fix does not change how motion functions are called, and both checkers judged it can be made with no robot ("none" or "low" risk). Many still deserve a look on the robot afterwards.

## Program lifecycle and competition modes

### LIFE-01
**Auton cut off mid-motion leaves the chassis motion lock held, so later motions hang forever**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/chassis.cpp:125](../../src/lemlib/chassis/chassis.cpp#L125)
- **What happens:** A sync motion (the team's usual `..., false)` style) runs its loop in the calling task. requestMotionStart() sets motionRunning=true and takes `pros::Mutex mutex`. That is a FreeRTOS mutex that records its owner: mutex_create jumps straight to xQueueCreateMutex(type 1). endMotion(), at the bottom of the motion, gives it back. When the competition mode changes, the PROS system daemon calls task_delete() on the competition task and creates a new one in the same static buffer. A task deleted inside the motion loop never reaches endMotion(). FreeRTOS does not release a deleted task's mutex, so the lock stays taken and motionRunning stays true. Nothing resets this: disabled() and opcontrol() onl…
- **When:** An auton written with sync calls is still inside a moveToPoint or turnToHeading when the 15 s period ends: a long routine, or moveToPose stalling short until its timeout. Or the competition switch is flipped mid-routine. Then, without restarting the program, any of these follows: the next match's or practice run's autonomous, an A+B test-run once…
- **Evidence:** chassis.cpp:120-125 `if (this->isInMotion()) this->motionQueued = true; else this->motionRunning = true; ... this->mutex.take(TIMEOUT_MAX);` and chassis.cpp:132-138 `this->motionRunning = this->motionQueued; ... this->mutex.give();` (only reached at the end of a motion). main.cpp:119 `void disabled() { stopMechanisms(); }`, main.cpp:132-136 and 13…
- **Suggested fix:** Guard, safe now and interface unchanged: in pidtuner onRun, refuse with "motion still running" when chassis.isInMotion() is true, and refuse the A+B test-run the same way (show it on the controller). After a cut-off motionRunning stays true, so this catches the stuck lock and keeps driving alive. Proper fix, library only with the motion interface unchanged: make the lock releasable after a period change. For example, replace the owner-tracked pr…
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** Guard (safe now, nothing in the interface changes): keep both parts as proposed. In pidtuner onRun, add `else if (chassis.isInMotion()) refuse = "motion stuck, restart";`. In the A+B branch of driverControl, if chassis.isInMotion(), show "MOTION STUCK" on the controller with a rumble and skip runAu…
- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Now: safe, no robot needed, interface unchanged. (1) In pidtuner onRun, add `else if (chassis.isInMotion()) refuse = "motion stuck: restart program";`. (2) In driverControl's A+B branch, before macros::cancel()/runAuton(), check chassis.isInMotion(). If it is true, show "MOTION STUCK" on the contro…
- **Flags:** severity: medium (reviewer said high); status: confirmed; fix size: medium; needs robot: no (small, clearly correct change); changes interface: no; already known: no

### LIFE-03
**Autonomous never sets the drive brake mode: COAST after boot, BRAKE after a driver period**

- **Where:** [src/main.cpp:132](../../src/main.cpp#L132)
- **What happens:** autonomous() calls stopMechanisms() and runAuton() and never touches the drive brake mode. Only two places set it: driverControl() (COAST at the start, BRAKE once 90 s have passed, and nothing switches it back) and the PID tab, which uses BRAKE during a test and then restores the previous mode. disabled() leaves it alone. So the first autonomous after power-on runs in the motors' default mode, which is COAST. Any autonomous after a driver period that lasted more than 90 s runs in BRAKE. Every LemLib motion ends with move(0), so how the robot stops after each move depends on which matches came before. The PID tab says it tunes in BRAKE because 'an auton would not' roll on. That assumption is…

### LIFE-04
**A mode change mid-printf leaves PROS's serial write lock held; every later printf hangs**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:117](../../src/controls.cpp#L117)
- **What happens:** PROS's system daemon runs at priority 14 and the competition task at priority 8. On a mode change the daemon deletes the competition task wherever it was interrupted. The only checks are task_get_state in {ready, blocked, suspended}, then task_delete, then task_create_static into the same competition_task_buffer and stack. FreeRTOS task_delete does not release mutexes the task holds: it removes the task from its lists and runs prvDeleteTCB, and that is all. Every stdout or stderr write goes through ser_write_r. That function takes write_mtx with TIMEOUT_MAX (stdout's non-blocking flag byte is 0), copies the COBS frame into the 2047-byte write stream with stream_buf_send (also TIMEOUT_MAX),…
- **When:** Field control or a competition switch changes mode while a competition task is between mutex_take and mutex_give inside a printf. Today that means a driver L1 press or claw catch at the exact moment driver changes to disabled. This can be at the end of a match, at a field pause, or if the program is left running into the next match. In the followi…
- **Evidence:** From disassembly of firmware/libpros.a: - system_daemon.c.o, do_background_operations: `bl port_mutex_take_all; bl ser_output_flush; bl rtos_suspend_all; bl vexBackgroundProcessing; bl rtos_resume_all; bl vdml_background_processing; b port_mutex_give_all`. - _system_daemon_task: `7c: bl do_background_operations; 80: bl competition_get_status; ...…
- **Suggested fix:** Keep stdio out of opcontrol, autonomous and disabled. Add a small log helper that snprintf's into a fixed-size slot and calls pros::c::queue_append(q, slot, 0). A FreeRTOS queue uses only critical sections, holds no mutex, and drops the message when full. Drain it with a logger task started in initialize() that does the printf. Use the helper at controls.cpp:117, :122, :317, :332 and main.cpp:128. fdctl(STDOUT_FILENO, SERCTL_NOBLKWRITE) is not a…
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** The proposed logger is sound. queue_append is `xQueueGenericSend(q,item,0,0)`, which uses only critical sections and cannot be interrupted halfway by a deletion. snprintf, including %f, is safe in competition tasks because PROS's `__malloc_lock` is `rtos_suspend_all`, so the daemon cannot delete a…
- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Lowest-effort fix: skip stdio inside competition tasks whenever field control or a switch is attached. In a match no terminal is reading the output anyway, and with nothing attached the mode cannot change. - Wrap the opcontrol event prints at controls.cpp:117, :122 and :154 (the claim missed :154,…
- **Flags:** severity: low (reviewer said medium); status: confirmed; fix size: small; needs robot: no (small, clearly correct change); changes interface: no; already known: no
- **Found by:** extra probe: kernel-locks-at-task-deletion

### LIFE-05
**A+B test run cancels a macro without waiting, so the macro's exit commands override the routine's first moves**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:316](../../src/controls.cpp#L316)
- **What happens:** autonomous() goes through stopMechanisms(): macros::cancel(), wait up to 100 ms for the macro task to exit, then stopAll(). The A+B test run calls macros::cancel() and starts runAuton() straight away. The macro task notices the cancel at its next 10 ms check and runs its exit path: homeCascade does cascade.brake(), homeRoller does rollerTurn.brake(), unloadMotion and pickUpMotion do intakeStop(). Those land after the routine's first non-blocking mechanism commands, so a test run behaves differently from the same routine under field control.
- **When:** The driver presses B (ResetAll: homes the cascade for up to 4 s, then the roller) to reset the robot before a test, then immediately holds A and presses B. The routine starts with cascade.move_absolute(...) or intake(100), and about 10 ms later homeCascade's abort path brakes the cascade, so the lift never moves in the test.

- **Suggested fix:** Move stopMechanisms() somewhere both files can call it (for example a macros::stopAndWait() or a function in subsystems) and call it in the A+B branch in place of macros::cancel().

### LIFE-09
**PROS's daemon flushes a deleted task's half-written printf with no timeout**

- **Where:** [src/main.cpp:128](../../src/main.cpp#L128)
- **What happens:** When the daemon deletes a competition task, prvDeleteTCB runs in the daemon's own context and calls _reclaim_reent on the task's reent. If that task ever used stdio, __sdidinit is set, so newlib calls __cleanup. That is _cleanup_r, which runs _fwalk_reent(_fclose_r) over the task's own stdin, stdout and stderr. This newlib keeps per-task FILEs, and stdout is line-buffered (flags 9). _fclose_r calls __sflush_r, which writes any bytes still in that task's private buffer: _write(1), then ser_write_r, with write_mtx and stream_buf_send both waiting forever. This happens inside the daemon, which is the only code that drains the stream buffer (ser_output_flush is called only from do_background_op…

### RACE-01
**A queued motion that finds motionRunning false returns while still holding the mutex, so every later motion blocks forever**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/motions/moveToPoint.cpp:12](../../src/lemlib/chassis/motions/moveToPoint.cpp#L12)
- **What happens:** requestMotionStart (chassis.cpp:120-125) sets motionQueued or motionRunning, then calls mutex.take(TIMEOUT_MAX). Every motion file then does `if (!this->motionRunning) return;` (moveToPoint.cpp:12, moveToPose.cpp:12, turnToHeading.cpp:12, turnToPoint.cpp:12, swingToHeading.cpp:13, swingToPoint.cpp:13, pursuit.cpp follow) without calling endMotion or mutex.give(). The pros::Mutex is a plain non-recursive FreeRTOS mutex, so from then on any task's take, including the holder's own, waits forever. motionRunning is false at that point in three cases: (1) cancelAllMotions ran while a motion was queued; this is the documented use, see the example at chassis.hpp:860-873. (2) Two tasks queued behind…

- **Where:** [include/lemlib/logger/buffer.hpp:68](../../include/lemlib/logger/buffer.hpp#L68)
- **What happens:** Members are built in declaration order: bufferFunc, buffer, mutex, task, rate. Buffer's constructor starts the pros::Task in its initialiser list (buffer.cpp:9). `rate` has no initialiser and is only assigned by setRate(50) in BufferedStdout's constructor body, after Buffer's constructor returns. ### RACE-05
**infoSink() changes a shared_ptr count without atomics; logger is not thread-safe**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/logger/logger.cpp:4](../../src/lemlib/logger/logger.cpp#L4)
- **What happens:** infoSink() and telemetrySink() return the static shared_ptr by value, so every log call increments and then decrements the control block's use count. This toolchain's thread model is 'single', so libstdc++ uses _Lock_policy _S_single, and the count is changed with plain ldr/add/str. PROS's weak __cxa_guard_acquire is a plain byte test, so first-use construction of the function-local statics (infoSink, telemetrySink, bufferedStdout) is not protected either. If two tasks call infoSink() together and one is preempted between the load and the store, an increment is lost. When both copies are released the count reaches 0, the InfoSink is deleted while the static still points to it, and the next…
- **When:** Team code logs through lemlib::infoSink() from a second task (e.g. the macros or pidtuner task) while a motion loop is logging in another, and a tick or a higher-priority wake-up lands inside the few-instruction window. Rare, but it gets more likely the more tasks log.

- **Suggested fix:** Have infoSink()/telemetrySink() return `const std::shared_ptr<InfoSink>&` (or InfoSink&). Every call site `infoSink()->debug(...)` compiles unchanged, no count is touched, and the inc/dec drops out of every 10 ms loop. Correct the 'thread-safe' comment, or build the statics once from initialize() before other tasks start.

## Timing and loops

### TIMING-01
**Brake-after-90 s is timed from opcontrol entry: never fires in 60 s skills, resets after a field pause**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:291](../../src/controls.cpp#L291)
- **What happens:** driverControl() records start = pros::millis() on entry and switches the drive to BRAKE after BRAKE_AFTER_MS = 90000, assuming a 105 s match driver period. A 60 s driver-skills run never reaches it. If the referee pauses and resumes the match, PROS restarts opcontrol: driverControl() sets COAST again and restarts the 90 s count, so BRAKE comes 90 s after the resume, usually never.

### CTRL-03
**Placeholder or unknown routine shows 'RUN #1' then '#1 0.0s' as if a routine ran**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:311](../../src/controls.cpp#L311)
- **What happens:** The test run's "nothing picked" check only tests `routine == 999`. Tapping the NONE placeholder on a selector tab sets auton to 1, -1 or 101 (selector.cpp:149/180/211). runAuton() sends those, and any number with no case, to `default:`, which only printf's 'no routine selected'. driverControl() can't tell, so the controller shows 'RUN #1' and then '#1 0.0s'. Nothing clears line 0 afterwards, so that text, or the 'NO ROUTINE PICKE' text, stays on the controller until the next test run, including into later periods while the program keeps running.

### CTRL-05
**'NO ROUTINE PICKED' rumble is sent microseconds after the text and is dropped**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:314](../../src/controls.cpp#L314)
- **What happens:** In the test-run path with routine 999, set_text(0,0,...) and rumble("-") are called back to back with no delay. controller_rumble is controller_set_text on line 3 (disassembled), so both go through vexControllerTextSet, which applies the one-message-per-50-ms limit (10 ms on a wired link). The second call, the rumble, falls inside that window and is refused. PROS returns PROS_ERR with errno EAGAIN, and the code ignores it. The rumble is the only cue the driver can feel; their eyes are usually on the robot or the brain, not the controller screen. The same function already knows the rule: line 334 does `pros::delay(60); // the controller drops updates under 50ms apart` before the second text.
- **When:** With no routine picked on the brain (auton == 999) and no field control: hold A and press B. The text appears; no rumble is felt.
- **Evidence:** controls.cpp:313 `master.set_text(0, 0, "NO ROUTINE PICKED ");` then :314 `master.rumble("-");` with nothing in between; :334 `pros::delay(60); // the controller drops updates under 50ms apart`. libpros.a: `<controller_rumble>: mov r3,r1; mov r2,#0; mov r1,#3; b controller_set_text`; controller_set_text: `bl vexControllerTextSet ... cmp r4,#0 ...…
- **Suggested fix:** Send the rumble first, then pros::delay(60), then the text. This path already blocks the loop and is only reachable off the field.

- **What happens:** dumpLog() writes DUMP_ROWS_PER_CHUNK=8 rows, then waits DUMP_CHUNK_DELAY_MS=10. A row averages 29.2 B of text plus about 6 B of PROS framing (COBS byte, 'sout' header, 0x00): stdout is line-buffered, so every row goes out as its own packet. That is about 282 B every ~10.3 ms, or roughly 27 KB/s: 2.4x a 115200-baud budget (11.5 KB/s) and about 10 to 18x what the radio delivered. The PROS kernel itself does not drop data (it blocks writers when its 2047-byte buffer is full and only hands VEXos what vexSerialWriteFree allows), so the loss happens after the brain, on the radio/controller route, and that route keeps only the newest ~4 KB. ### SERIAL-02
**A printf can freeze opcontrol, the screen or the colour task forever if the serial link stops draining**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:117](../../src/controls.cpp#L117)
- **What happens:** PROS write path (libpros ser_write_r): takes write_mtx with TIMEOUT_MAX, COBS-encodes the line, then calls stream_buf_send with timeout TIMEOUT_MAX while still holding the mutex, and only gives the mutex after the line is queued. stream_buf_send loops until the whole packet fits (stream_buf_get_unused >= len), sleeping on task_notify_wait. The only thing that frees space is ser_output_flush. The system daemon (priority 14) calls it every 2 ms, and it moves at most vexSerialWriteFree(1) bytes into VEXos. All four standard files have flags byte 0 (blocking). The documented way out, fdctl(fd, SERCTL_NOBLKWRITE, NULL), is a no-op in this libpros: ser_ctl returns 0 for 12 and 13 without touching…

This only matters IF VEXos then reports no free space; that is unverified. Untethered match use is evidently safe, or every PROS program that prints in a loop would freeze. T…

The function is declared extern "C" and is in libpros's VEX SDK jump table. Use it for the opcontrol, macros, colourToggle, selector and auton prints. Leave dumpLog as it is: it runs in its own task, and it should stay lossless over the…

Plug in the cable, run 'pros mu', leave the terminal closed for more than 60 s while [optical] prints, then press L1 and tap the alliance button and check whether the drive and screen still respond. ### SERIAL-06
**Lines can't splice between tasks: each task has its own stdout, sent one packet per line**

- **Where:** [src/pidtuner.cpp:184](../../src/pidtuner.cpp#L184)
- **What happens:** The earlier finding ('stdout has no lock: printf from two tasks can splice or repeat lines') does not hold for this build. PROS builds newlib reentrant per task: vTaskSwitchContext stores _impure_ptr, and task deletion calls _reclaim_reent. __sinit sets up _stdin, _stdout and _stderr inside each task's own _reent, so every task has its own stdout FILE and buffer. That FILE is line-buffered with 1024 B: ser_fstat_r reports S_IFCHR (0x2000), so __swhatbuf_r returns 1024 and says it could be a tty, ser_isatty_r returns 1, and __smakebuf_r sets __SLBF. Each newline therefore triggers exactly one _write, and ser_write_r turns that into one COBS packet ('sout' + line + 0x00) queued while holding…
- **When:** Nothing to trigger. The point is to stop a planned fix: adding a mutex around printf would change nothing. The splices only happen on the controller-radio route, from lost bytes, and pacing (the known dump findings) is the lever.
- **Evidence:** libc __swhatbuf_r: 'and.w r3, r3, #61440 ; sub.w r3, r3, #8192 ; clz ; lsrs #5' (couldbetty = S_ISCHR) and 'mov.w r2, #1024'. __smakebuf_r: 'bl _isatty_r ; ... orr.w r3, r3, #1' (__SLBF). __sinit(r4 = reent): 'ldr r0, [r4, #4] ; bl std ... ldr r0, [r4, #8] ; bl std ... ldr r0, [r4, #12] ; bl std'. tasks.c.o references _impure_ptr in the scheduler…
- **Suggested fix:** Change the earlier finding and any plan based on it: no printf lock is needed. If a future line could exceed 1 KB, split it. To check whether the brain itself ever drops bytes on the radio, make a test build that calls serctl(SERCTL_ACTIVATE, (void*)0x6762646b) in initialize() and capture with pros terminal --raw. The kdbg warning is invisible in normal terminal mode.
- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Drop the planned printf mutex and correct the earlier 'stdout has no lock' finding: printf and fwrite to stdout use a per-task, line-buffered FILE, and each line goes out as one COBS packet sent under write_mtx. Add two notes: (a) std::cout is the exception. It is one shared FILE with no lock, so k…

### SERIAL-08
**Colour thresholds have no hysteresis, so toggleB and the terminal chatter near a boundary**

- **Where:** [src/subsystems.cpp:173](../../src/subsystems.cpp#L173)
- **What happens:** Every test is a single hard threshold: proximity >= 150, saturation >= 0.2 (blue blocks read 0.26-0.30), and hue edges at 25/335, 40/75 and 180/260. Each change of the result sends a new motor command and prints a line. A reading that wobbles across a threshold flips toggleB between full speed and HOLD brake on each sensor update, up to about every 20 ms. Each flip prints a '[toggle] ...' line and re-homes toggleA. That is up to about 50 lines and 50 hard start/stops a second. printOptical also adds a 45-byte line twice a second for the whole program. That output shares the controller-radio serial link that already loses PID-tab log rows.
- **When:** A block sits or slides slowly at the edge of the sensor's range, with proximity hovering near 150, or a red block whose hue reads around 20-30. toggleB jerks on and off, and the terminal floods during a tuning session or a PIDLOG dump.
- **Evidence:** subsystems.cpp:86-93 single-value thresholds; :173-184 `if (want != spinning) { ... toggleB.move_velocity ... toggleB.brake(); ... std::printf("[toggle] %s alliance, sees %s: %s\n", ...` ; :119-125 printOptical every 500 ms, unconditionally. HANDOFF: through the controller's radio most runs lose rows.
- **Suggested fix:** Add hysteresis: switch on at proximity >= 150 and off below about 130, with a similar margin on saturation and hue. Or require 2-3 equal readings before changing state. Rate-limit the '[toggle]' print, and put printOptical behind a debug flag that is off by default, like the rumble flag in selector.cpp.
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** First and with no behaviour risk: put printOptical behind a `constexpr bool PRINT_OPTICAL = false;` flag (same pattern as RUMBLE_UNTIL_PICKED in selector.cpp), to be turned on while calibrating the thresholds. ### DRIVER-01
**Holding UP/DOWN while R1 or B starts makes the macro open the claw or zero the lift mid-air**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:237](../../src/controls.cpp#L237)
- **What happens:** cascadeControls() writes cascade.move_velocity(+/-200 rpm) on every tick that UP/DOWN is held, and cascade.brake() on release. Only a fresh press (the `grabbed` edge) cancels a macro. placeRelease() (R1) and homeCascade() (B) send their downward move_velocity once and then only watch position: 'under 2 deg moved in 40 ms (R1) / 100 ms (B) after 200 ms' counts as landed. While UP is held, the driver's +200 rpm replaces the macro's single -200 within 10 ms. When UP is let go, the release brake (HOLD mode) stops the lift wherever it is, and the stall detector reads that as landed. R1 then runs setClaw(true) and outtakes, dropping what it holds above the pin. B runs cascade.tare_position_all(),…
- **When:** The driver lifts with UP and presses R1 to place before lifting the thumb off UP. A few tens of ms of overlap is enough, and so is pressing UP and R1 in the same tick: cancel() runs first, then R1's request starts PlaceRelease. Likewise B pressed while UP or DOWN is held, then the D-pad released before the lift reaches the bottom.

Keep a `manualCascade` flag: set it on an UP/DOWN press, clear it when R1, R2 or B queues a macro, and send the hold and release-brake commands only while it is set. The driver re-presses UP or DOWN to take back over, which cancels the macro as it does now. The simpler alternative is to ignore R1/B while UP or DOWN is held.

- **What happens:** antiTipScale() takes the lift height from cascade.get_position(), i.e. port 8 only. When port 8 cannot be read, it returns PROS_ERR_F (INFINITY). The `!std::isfinite(lift)` branch then returns 1.0, full speed. Meanwhile UP and DOWN still move the lift on port 9 alone, because move_velocity goes to the whole group. ### MACRO-02
**R1 and R2 presses are silently dropped while any other macro is still running**

- **Where:** [src/controls.cpp:248](../../src/controls.cpp#L248)
- **What happens:** request(macro, false) returns false whenever isBusy() is true (macros.cpp:331-332), and macroControls ignores the return value. R1 never pre-empts. R2 pre-empts only another CycleHeight. CycleHeight stays busy until spinTo gets within 2 deg or hits its travel-based timeout (about 0.9-2 s). ResetAll stays busy through the lift homing (travel + 300 ms, up to 4 s) and then the whole roller homing (approach + 350-1500 ms push), even though the lift is already at CASCADE_LOAD by then. A press in those windows does nothing: no queue, no print, no rumble.

Not a rumble, which would add controller traffic.

## Mechanisms (subsystems)

### MECH-01
**A cascade motor power cycle loses the lift's zero; with one motor reset the pair fight on every preset**

- **Where:** [src/subsystems.cpp:19](../../src/subsystems.cpp#L19)
- **What happens:** The lift's zero is set exactly twice: cascade.tare_position_all() in init (subsystems.cpp:19) and B's homeCascade (macros.cpp:169). A V5 motor's encoder is incremental, so when a motor loses power the position it reports afterwards is no longer measured from the bottom stop. Its count starts again where the shaft is when power returns. Even if VEXos kept the tare offset, the lift sags while that motor cannot hold. Either way the reading is off by roughly the lift height at the moment power came back (likely; exact value needs-robot). Verified by disassembly, MotorGroup::move_absolute loops `ldrsb r0,[r5],#1; bl motor_move_absolute`: the same absolute number goes to motor 8 and motor 9, each…
- **When:** Mid-match a cascade motor's smart cable on port 8 or 9 is knocked loose for a moment, or ESD resets the motor, while the lift is at say 900 deg. Port 9: the next R2 press makes the two lift motors push against each other, heat up and stall short. Port 8: the lift reads near 0 while it is high, so the anti-tip limit is off and the presets overshoot.
- **Evidence:** vdml_motorgroup.cpp.o MotorGroup::move_absolute(double,long): `40: ldrsb r0,[r5],#1` / `50: bl motor_move_absolute` (loop over ports, same r2/r3 target); MotorGroup::get_position(unsigned char): `44: ldrsb r0,[r3,r1]` / `4c: b motor_get_position`. robot-config.cpp:48 `pros::MotorGroup cascade({ports::CASCADE_A, ports::CASCADE_B}, ...)` with CASCAD…
- **Suggested fix:** Keep the interface. In the driver loop, about 4 times a second, read cascade.get_position_all(). The two coupled motors should agree to within backlash; if they differ by more than ~20 deg, or either reads non-finite, latch a 'lift frame lost' flag. Print it, put 'LIFT: PRESS B' on the controller, and have spinTo/moveTo for the cascade refuse absolute moves until homeCascade clears the flag. Do the same for rollerTurn by comparing against its la…
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** Keep every interface, and keep presets working. The changes: 1. antiTipScale: use the larger of the finite values from cascade.get_position_all(), not get_position(). If port 8 resets with the lift up, port 9 still reads high and anti-tip stays on. This is a one-line, low-risk change. 2. Lift check…
- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Keep it additive and warning-only, with no change to how moveTo or spinTo behave and no thresholds to tune: (1) In driverControl's loop, read cascade.get_position_all() and rollerTurn.get_position() every tick. ### MECH-02

- **Where:** [include/subsystems.hpp:84](../../include/subsystems.hpp#L84)
- **What happens:** rollerMainFor() only records a stop time. The stop happens in rollerMainTimeout(), and its only caller is driverControl()'s loop. While that loop is not running (autonomous(), or the blocking runAuton() in the A+B test), the timer is never checked, so rollerMain keeps spinning until something else commands it. stopMechanisms() at the end of auton is the backstop. ### MECH-07
**spinToNear leaves the lift aimed at 0 after stalling above it, so it keeps pushing into the stop**

- **Where:** [src/subsystems.cpp:286](../../src/subsystems.cpp#L286)
- **What happens:** spinToNear returns true on a stall (and on timeout) without braking or re-targeting. The motor keeps its move_absolute target. Its own comment expects 'the cascade sitting on its stop a little off 0', so after pickUpMotion the cascade's internal position loop keeps driving toward CASCADE_MIN=0 against the hard stop or an obstruction. It holds there until the next cascade command. yStart() also aims the cascade at 0 for the whole drive to the pin. A V5 motor holding an unreachable target draws steady current and heats up.
- **When:** The lift's zero has slipped a few degrees (backlash or a skipped tooth since the last B), or the lift lands on a game element. After a pickup the lift keeps pressing down until something else commands it. Today that is only reachable from future autons (yFinish/yStart); the PickUp macros are not bound to buttons.

### LVGL-05

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/selector.cpp:332](../../src/lemlib/selector.cpp#L332)
- **What happens:** The handlers are registered on LV_EVENT_VALUE_CHANGED, and the matrices only set LV_BTNMATRIX_CTRL_CHECKABLE (line 331). In LVGL 8.3 a btnmatrix sends VALUE_CHANGED on PRESSED (at touch-down), again on PRESSING whenever the finger slides onto another button, and on every LONG_PRESSED_REPEAT (every 100 ms after 400 ms). - **When:** Someone picks AI routine -2. Before the match they swipe across to the Qual tab and back to check it, and the swipe starts on a Qual button. - **Evidence:** include/liblvgl/widgets/lv_btnmatrix.h:41 `LV_BTNMATRIX_CTRL_CLICK_TRIG = 0x0100, /**< 1: Send LV_EVENT_VALUE_CHANGE on CLICK, 0: Send LV_EVENT_VALUE_CHANGE on PRESS*/`. ### LEMLIB-02
**TeamChassis::turnToHeading picks its small/big-turn rule from the heading when the call is queued, not when the turn starts**

- **Where:** [src/robot-config.cpp:143](../../src/robot-config.cpp#L143)
- **What happens:** turnSize = |angleError(theta, getPose().theta)| is computed before lemlib::Chassis::turnToHeading is called, and that base call blocks in requestMotionStart until the previous motion ends. LemLib motions default to async=true (and so does TeamChassis's turnToHeading, robot-config.hpp:72). A routine's next line therefore runs about 10 ms after the previous motion started, while the robot is still near its old heading. So the minSpeed 51 rule (under 45 deg, which also turns on LemLib's exit-at-crossing) and the cap at 80 (100 deg and over) are chosen from the wrong turn size.
- **When:** `chassis.turnToHeading(90, 1000); chassis.turnToHeading(-30, 1000);` The second call measures about 30 deg from the old heading, so it gets minSpeed 51 and no cap. The real turn is 120 deg, run uncapped up to 127 and ended by the minSpeed exit the moment it crosses -30, so it overshoots by its coast. Or `turnToHeading(180); turnToHeading(150);`: t…
- **Evidence:** robot-config.cpp:143-147 `const float turnSize = std::fabs(lemlib::angleError(theta, getPose().theta, false)); if (turnSize < SMALL_TURN_DEG && params.minSpeed == 0) params.minSpeed = SMALL_TURN_MIN_SPEED; ... lemlib::Chassis::turnToHeading(theta, timeout, params, async);`. turnToHeading.cpp:58 `if (params.minSpeed != 0 && sgn(deltaTheta) != sgn(p…
- **Suggested fix:** At the top of TeamChassis::turnToHeading, wait for the running motion before measuring: `while (isInMotion()) pros::delay(10);`, then compute turnSize. The base call already blocks the caller until then, so timing changes by at most 10 ms and the calling interface is unchanged. Update the stale comment at robot-config.hpp:67.

Also note there that a cancelMotion() from another task during the wait would resume the cancelled motion and drop this turn (nothing does that today). If…

### LEMLIB-06
**SMALL_TURN_DEG = 45 is the most common auton angle, so IMU noise picks the turn's regime**

- **Where:** [include/robot-config.hpp:75](../../include/robot-config.hpp#L75)
- **What happens:** TeamChassis::turnToHeading adds minSpeed 51, and with it LemLib's 'stop at the target crossing' exit, when turnSize < 45. turnSize comes from the live heading. A nominal 45 deg turn therefore measures 45 plus or minus a few hundredths (IMU drift) or tenths (where the previous turn ended, within about 1 deg, with + turns about 0.5 deg short per HANDOFF). One 45 deg turn can run the normal PID and the next the min-speed bang-bang regime. The team already avoided the same trap at the other boundary: BIG_TURN_DEG is 100, 'not 90, so a 90 deg turn that starts slightly off (90.4) isn't capped'. ### LEMLIB-07
**LemLib's IMU calibration wait has no time limit; a stuck IMU would hang initialize() for good**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/chassis.cpp:58](../../src/lemlib/chassis/chassis.cpp#L58)
- **What happens:** calibrateIMU() polls `while (status != error && is_calibrating())` with no deadline after a non-blocking reset(). PROS's own imu_reset_blocking() gives up after 3000 ms with EAGAIN, but LemLib does not use it. If the IMU keeps its calibrating flag set (a faulty or glitching IMU), initialize() never returns: the logo stays up, the selector and PID tab are never built, subsystems::init() never zeroes the mechanisms, and PROS does not start the competition callbacks (autonomous/opcontrol) until initialize() finishes. The 5-attempt retry only covers attempts that end, which in practice means the unplugged case (status error).
- **When:** An IMU that reports calibrating indefinitely at power-on. Rare, but if it happens the robot is dead for the match, with only a frozen logo as the clue.
- **Evidence:** chassis.cpp:57-58 `do pros::delay(10); while (sensors.imu->get_status() != pros::ImuStatus::error && sensors.imu->is_calibrating());`. Disassembly of imu_reset_blocking in libpros.a: `movw r7, #2999` loop bound, then errno 11 (EAGAIN). Imu::is_calibrating returns (status != 0xFF) & 1.
- **Suggested fix:** Bound each attempt's wait to about 3 s, as PROS does, and count a timeout as a failed attempt so the existing retry and fallback path runs.
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** Inside calibrateIMU only (no change to the public API), give each attempt a deadline: record `uint32_t start = pros::millis();` after reset() and add `&& pros::millis() - start < 3500` to the while condition. - **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Add a per-attempt deadline and leave the retry and fallback logic as it is. In calibrateIMU: `const uint32_t start = pros::millis(); do pros::delay(10); while (sensors.imu->get_status() != pros::ImuStatus::error && sensors.imu->is_calibrating() && pros::millis() - start < 3000);`. ### LEMLIB-09

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/chassis.cpp:121](../../src/lemlib/chassis/chassis.cpp#L121)
- **What happens:** requestMotionStart() sets motionQueued = true when a motion is already running, then blocks in mutex.take(TIMEOUT_MAX). When the autonomous period ends, PROS deletes the autonomous task. The waiting task disappears, but motionQueued stays true. The running async motion's own task survives the mode change (known) and finishes on its timeout. Its endMotion() then does `motionRunning = motionQueued` (true), clears motionQueued and gives the mutex. Now no motion runs, but isInMotion() returns true. Later sync motions keep it latched: their requestMotionStart() sees isInMotion(), sets motionQueued = true itself, takes the free mutex, runs normally, and its endMotion() sets motionRunning = true a…

It cannot release a mutex still held by a killed sync motion (a separate known issue). Motion calls do not change.

### LEMLIB-14

- **What happens:** Before the zone, lateralError = pose.distance(carrot) * sgn(cos). On the approach line the carrot is lead*d in front of the target, so the error is only (1-lead)*d, which is 0.4*d at the default lead 0.6. ### LEMLIB-15

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/motions/moveToPose.cpp:92](../../src/lemlib/chassis/motions/moveToPose.cpp#L92)
- **What happens:** Once close, the angular error targets the final heading (87) and lateralError = d*cos(angle between heading and bearing to target) (92). The robot can only move along its heading. Any sideways offset at entry, or any created by yaw while braking, cannot be removed. The lateral exits see only the along-heading part, so the motion is declared settled with the offset left. A moveToPose that starts inside 7.5 in skips the boomerang altogether: a target to the side of the final heading gives lateralError close to 0 and the robot does not move. - **Evidence:** Replica on the current file (slip cap applied only before the zone): sideways offset at exit 0.18-0.28 in at lead 0.6, up to 1.77 in at lead 0.3, up to 2.52 in at lead 0.1, while the along-heading error was 0.08 in. moveToPose(5,0,0) from (0,0,0): ended at t=520 ms without moving, 5.00 in from the target, 'settled'.
- **Suggested fix:** Inside the zone, blend the angular target from the bearing to the target toward the final heading as d shrinks (or keep steering to a carrot until d is very small). Alternatively, measure the small exit on the full distance. This changes behaviour and needs robot testing.

### LEMLIB-17
**A malformed mid-file path line silently truncates the path, and follow() drives the stub with no stop point**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/motions/pursuit.cpp:76](../../src/lemlib/chassis/motions/pursuit.cpp#L76)
- **What happens:** getData stops at the first line that does not split into exactly 3 fields on ", " (for example a blank line, "x,y,v" without spaces, or a 4-field line). It logs an error and returns the points read so far. follow() only rejects an empty vector (line 217), so it runs the partial path. That path has no zero-speed point and no 20 in extension. The end condition at line 254 can never be met, and once the lookahead circle leaves the last segment, lookaheadPoint returns the stale lookahead. The robot passes the last point at that point's speed and keeps driving in a wide arc until timeout.
- **When:** Someone hand-edits a jerryio file (to tweak a speed or point) and leaves a blank line, or types a comma without the following space, partway through the point list. The only sign is an ERROR line on the terminal, which is not attached during a match.

### LEMLIB-24
**Backward turnToPoint/swingToPoint report 180 deg travelled at start, so waitUntil returns at once**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/motions/turnToPoint.cpp:42](../../src/lemlib/chassis/motions/turnToPoint.cpp#L42)
- **What happens:** startTheta is the raw heading (line 24), but when forwards=false pose.theta is replaced by theta-180 (line 39). distTraveled = |angleError(theta-180, startTheta)| is therefore 180 on the first loop and falls as the robot turns. waitUntil(d) returns as soon as distTraveled > d (chassis.cpp:109-113). swingToPoint.cpp:25/47/50 does the same. Separately, for every turn distTraveled is the shortest angle from the start, which tops out at 180: waitUntil(d >= 180) never fires before the end, and on a forced turn longer than 180 the value rises and then falls.

### LEMLIB-25
**LemLib's log buffer holds its mutex while printing, so log callers wait on serial**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/logger/buffer.cpp:34](../../src/lemlib/logger/buffer.cpp#L34)
- **What happens:** Buffer::taskLoop takes `mutex`, calls bufferFunc(buffer.at(0)), then pops and gives the mutex. bufferFunc is std::cout << text << std::flush, a blocking serial write through write_mtx and the stream buffer, both with infinite waits. pushToBuffer takes the same mutex, so every LemLib log call waits for any serial write in progress. With debug logging turned on for tuning, each motion loop iteration (moveToPoint.cpp:106, turnToHeading.cpp:73, moveToPose.cpp:141) can stall behind the Buffer task's write. 2. ### LEMLIB-26

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/pid.cpp:25](../../src/lemlib/pid.cpp#L25)
- **What happens:** reset() zeroes prevError and prevPrevError. The new two-sample derivative (error - prevPrevError)/2 therefore equals error/2 on BOTH the first and second update of every motion, a kick of kD*error/2 held for two loops. The old one-sample code kicked kD*error for one loop. The kick is hidden only where the output is slewed. turnToHeading/turnToPoint/swingTo* slew only while |deltaTheta| > 20, so turns under 20 deg get no slew. moveToPose never slews angularOut. With the turn gains (4.8/0.25/42) a 10 deg turn outputs 258, 253, then 30: two loops clamped to maxSpeed 127. Before the fix it was 470 for one loop, then 44.

### LEMLIB-27

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/util.cpp:21](../../src/lemlib/util.cpp#L21)
- **What happens:** This is the known bug (target sanitised twice, position never). What it adds: turnToHeading passes the unwrapped heading, while turnToPoint and the swings wrap it with fmod to (-360, 360). A forced turn is computed correctly only while the heading lies in (target-360, target+360]. Outside that range the result can point the opposite way: CW to 90 from 470 gives -20 (a 20 deg CCW turn instead of 340 CW), and CCW to 10 from -400 gives +50 (CW). It can also be 360 too big: CW to 300 from -100 gives 400. Any net CCW rotation below -60 deg is enough, e.g. setPose(0,0,0) then turning to -100. In the 400 case the settling latch stops the robot at the real crossing, but the PID has been working on…
- **When:** `chassis.setPose(0,0,0); chassis.turnToHeading(-100, 1500); chassis.turnToHeading(300, 1500, {.direction = lemlib::AngularDirection::CW_CLOCKWISE});`. The second turn is a 40 deg turn that the PID treats as 400 deg. A skills route that builds up more than one net revolution gets wrong-direction forced turns.
- **Evidence:** util.cpp:20-21 `target = sanitizeAngle(target, radians); target = sanitizeAngle(target, radians);` turnToHeading.cpp:53 passes raw `pose.theta`. Scratch: "CW to 90 from 470: -20", "CW to 300 from -100: 400 (shortest 40)", "CCW to 10 from -400: 50".

### ODOM-01
**An IMU dropout turns the pose into NaN until the next setPose: drives sit to timeout, turns 'finish' in 0.2 s**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/odom.cpp:87](../../src/lemlib/chassis/odom.cpp#L87)
- **What happens:** `imuRaw = degToRad(imu->get_rotation())` is not checked. PROS returns PROS_ERR_F = +inf when the IMU is unplugged, and also while it recalibrates after reconnecting (EAGAIN). On the first bad tick: deltaImu = inf, so heading = inf, avgHeading = inf, sin(inf) = NaN, and x and y become NaN. On the next tick inf - inf = NaN, so theta becomes NaN too. NaN never clears: once the IMU is back, prevImu becomes finite again, but heading = odomPose.theta + deltaImu stays NaN. Only setPose recovers it. In moveToPoint, fmax(NaN, 0) makes lateralOut 0, angularOut is NaN, and move(NaN) converts to 0 on ARM, so the motors stop; `close` is never true, so it runs to its timeout. In turnToHeading, ExitCondit…
- **When:** The IMU on port 13 blinks out for a moment (static from the field tiles, or a cable bump) during an auton or a PID tab session. The rest of the auton does nothing with the drive while the mechanisms keep firing, and every later PID tab run does nothing until reboot.
- **Evidence:** odom.cpp:87 `if (odomSensors.imu != nullptr) imuRaw = degToRad(odomSensors.imu->get_rotation());` and :119 `else if (odomSensors.imu != nullptr) heading += deltaImu;`. exitcondition.cpp:14 `if (std::fabs(input) > range) startTime = -1;` (NaN falls through to 'in range'). Host run, IMU returning inf from t=1000 during moveToPoint(0,48): `t=901 odom…
- **Suggested fix:** odom.cpp: if !std::isfinite(imuRaw), hold the heading for that tick (deltaImu = 0) and do not overwrite prevImu. On the first finite reading after an error, set prevImu to the new reading so the heading carries on instead of jumping (the IMU restarts near 0 after its own recalibration). ExitCondition::update: treat non-finite input as out of range (`if (!(std::fabs(input) <= range)) startTime = -1;`). The interface does not change.
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** odom.cpp update(): read `const double rot = odomSensors.imu->get_rotation(); const bool imuOk = std::isfinite(rot);`. Keep a `static bool prevImuValid`. - If imuOk: set `imuRaw = degToRad(rot)`. If `!prevImuValid`, rebase with `prevImu = imuRaw`, so the first good reading gives delta 0 and there is…

## Numbers and units

### NUM-01
**findClosest returns an uninitialised index when every distance is NaN**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/chassis/motions/pursuit.cpp:100](../../src/lemlib/chassis/motions/pursuit.cpp#L100)
- **What happens:** `int closestPoint;` is only assigned when `dist < closestDist`. If the pose is NaN, every hypot is NaN, every comparison is false, and the function returns an indeterminate value (undefined behaviour), which is then used in `pathPoints.at(closestPoint)`. In this build GCC happened to pick 0 (r7 set to #0), so the robot takes point 0's speed with NaN curvature. move(NaN) truncates to 0, and the robot sits still until timeout. A different build could return garbage, in which case at() throws std::out_of_range (uncaught, so the program terminates).
- **When:** The pose becomes NaN mid-auton: the IMU drops out, get_rotation returns PROS_ERR_F (inf), and odom.cpp turns inf - inf into NaN on the next update. Then follow() runs.
- **Evidence:** pursuit.cpp:100 `int closestPoint;`, :106-108 the only assignment, :112 `return closestPoint;`. objdump of findClosest in bin/cold.package.elf: `38743b8: mov r7, #0` ... `3874424: movgt r7, r4` ... `38743ec: mov r0, r7`. odom.cpp:87 `imuRaw = degToRad(odomSensors.imu->get_rotation());`, :94 `deltaImu = imuRaw - prevImu;`.
- **Suggested fix:** Initialise `int closestPoint = 0;`. Better: have follow() stop and log an error if the pose is not finite.

- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Make the one-line change `int closestPoint = 0;` at pursuit.cpp:100. It removes the UB and matches what the compiled binary already does (r7 starts at 0), so behaviour stays the same. Skip the finite-pose check in follow(): nothing calls follow(). Log the real cause as its own item: lemlib::update(…

### NUM-03
**sanitizeAngle returns exactly 360 for tiny negative angles; only the duplicate line in angleError hides it**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/util.cpp:15](../../src/lemlib/util.cpp#L15)
- **What happens:** sanitizeAngle computes fmod(fmod(a,360)+360,360) in double and returns float. For any a in (-1.5e-5, 0) deg (or (-2.4e-7, 0) rad) the double result 359.99999.. rounds to 360.0f, outside [0,360). angleError currently sanitises target twice (the known bug), and the second pass turns 360 into 0. That masks the problem. The obvious fix to the known bug (sanitise position on line 21 instead) exposes it: target 360, position 0 with a forced CW direction gives an error of 360 instead of 0.
- **When:** After the known angleError fix: turnToPoint or swingToPoint with a forced direction, where the computed target heading is a hair below 0 (robot almost exactly aimed). The first loop commands a full-circle error. The settling check catches it the next loop, so the result is a one-loop twitch rather than a spin.

A version with the position sanitised once: `fixed angleError(-2.5e-6, 0, CW) = 360`. Current code: `angleError(-1e-06,0,deg,CW)=0`, masked by util.cpp:20-21 sanitising target twice.

- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** When fixing util.cpp:21 (sanitise `position` instead of `target` a second time), the clamp is fine as a contract cleanup. ## Laptop tools

### TOOLS-01
**Stall test trusts motor rpm, which reads 13-55 rpm on stopped turns, so every short turn becomes ENDED_EARLY**

- **Where:** [tools/pidlog.py:443](../../tools/pidlog.py#L443)
- **What happens:** stalled_end = mean(|vL|,|vR|) over the last 150 ms < STALL_RPM (5). On the real robot, the V5 motor velocity at low speed holds stale or spiky values while about 1 V is applied: run #5 at 1100 ms reads vL=193, vR=-127 while the heading moves 0.01 deg per 10 ms, and vR=97 is held for 150 ms with the robot still. So a turn that stops short is never 'stalled'. classify() then takes ENDED_EARLY ('still creeping... approach too slow', kD -15%) instead of STOPS_SHORT (kP +12% / add kI). The 5 rpm threshold comes from the selftest sim, where stopped motors read 0 +/- 1.5 rpm (selftest line 153).
- **When:** Any TURN test that ends more than the small range short. This happened on run #2 (kI 0.15, kD 36).

### TOOLS-10
**Each run judged alone: + and - turns with the same gains get opposite advice; a run ending outside tolerance gets GOOD**

- **Where:** [tools/pidlog.py:583](../../tools/pidlog.py#L583)
- **What happens:** short = |err_at_end| > tol AND |final| > tol. A turn inside tolerance when LemLib ended but drifting out afterwards is GOOD ('Looks well tuned') even when the final error is over tolerance. ENDED_EARLY's 'approach too slow' wording is used even when rise_ms is far before end_ms, i.e. the robot reached the target and then backed out. No metric compares the error at end_ms with the final error, and runs are never paired by direction, although the PID tab alternates direction on purpose and HANDOFF says to judge from two runs.

### BUILD-07
**Deleting a LemLib or image source leaves its object in the archive, still linked**

- **Where:** [Makefile:96](../../Makefile#L96)
- **What happens:** $(LEMLIB_LIB): $(LEMLIB_OBJ) and $(COLD_IMG_LIB): $(COLD_IMG_OBJ) only rebuild when a listed object is newer than the archive. Deleting a source shrinks the list but leaves the archive up to date, so the old member stays in it. The cold link uses --whole-archive, so that stale code stays in the cold package until another LemLib file changes (the recipe's rm -f then rebuilds the archive). The same applies to removing an image from cold/.
- **When:** Someone deletes a LemLib file (for example pursuit.cpp, to save space) or removes dorito.c from cold/, then builds and uploads. The old code or picture is still in the cold package with no message.
- **Evidence:** Scratch: rm src/lemlib/chassis/motions/pursuit.cpp; make -n -> "Nothing to be done for `quick'"; `arm-none-eabi-ar t firmware/liblemlib.a | grep pursuit` -> pursuit.cpp.o.
- **Suggested fix:** Write the member list to a stamp file that is rewritten only when the list changes (via $(shell) with a cmp). Make each archive depend on its stamp, and archive $(filter %.o,$^).
- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** The proposed fix is sound. Some refinements: (1) Keep the stamps in bin/ (for example bin/lemlib.objlist and bin/coldimages.objlist) so `make clean` removes them along with the objects. ### DOCS-01
**CLAUDE.md says lv_* calls are safe anywhere in initialize(); the PID tab's call is the one that races**

- **Where:** [CLAUDE.md:64](../../CLAUDE.md#L64)
- **What happens:** The rule says lv_* may be called from 'LVGL event callbacks, an lv_timer, or initialize()' and that 'The PID tab follows this'. That holds only for the part of initialize() before its first blocking call, while no other task calls lv_*. pidtuner::init runs after selector::init has started tabWatcher, a priority-8 task that calls lv_* and time-slices with initialize(). After chassis.calibrate()'s waits, the display task may also be paused mid-frame. A teammate or agent following the rule as written could add more lv_* work after a delay or after tabWatcher starts.
- **When:** Someone adds lv_* code to initialize() after chassis.calibrate() or after selector::init, trusting CLAUDE.md, and widens the boot-time race.
- **Evidence:** CLAUDE.md:64-66: "PROS runs LVGL on its own task without a lock: call `lv_*` only from LVGL event callbacks, an `lv_timer`, or `initialize()`. The PID tab follows this." main.cpp:106-107 calls pidtuner::init after selector::init, which creates tabWatcher at selector.cpp:435.
- **Suggested fix:** Reword: initialize() is safe for lv_* only before its first blocking call and before any task that calls lv_* (tabWatcher) has started. Note that pidtuner::init currently runs after tabWatcher starts, until the ordering fix lands.

- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Reword CLAUDE.md:64-67 with the actual mechanism, and say that existing code already falls outside the safe zone. Suggested text: "PROS runs LVGL with no lock. The display daemon runs at priority 3. initialize() and tabWatcher both run at priority 8 and take turns at every 1 ms tick. Call lv_* only…

### DOCS-03
**The 'full hand spin returned X, Y to the start' check cannot detect wrong wheel offsets**

- **Where:** [docs/HANDOFF.md:16](../../docs/HANDOFF.md#L16)
- **What happens:** With a wrong offset, each tick adds an error of (configured - true offset) x dTheta in the robot's frame. Over a full revolution that error traces a circle and sums to zero, so X and Y return to the start for any offset error, even with a reversed horizontal wheel (the circle is then twice as big). The check therefore says nothing about the unmeasured 0.9 in and 2.55 in offsets. The error is largest after half a turn: X = 2 x (configured vertical offset - true) and Y = 2 x (configured horizontal offset - true). robot-config.cpp:73-74 also says only that X drifts during a turn on the spot, not that it comes back.
- **When:** The team reads the spin result as proof the odometry geometry is right; the offset error then shows up as position error after every turn that isn't a whole number of revolutions.
- **Evidence:** Host test, real update(): horizontal offset 2 in wrong gives `after 180 deg odom x=-0.000 y=4.000` and `after 360 deg x=-0.000 y=0.000`; vertical offset 1 in wrong gives `after 180 deg x=2.000 y=0.000` and `after 360 deg x=0 y=0`.
- **Suggested fix:** Replace the check in HANDOFF, the TUNING_RUNBOOK measurements list and the robot-config comment with: from setPose(0,0,0), turnToHeading(180) on the spot; X/2 is the vertical offset error and Y/2 the horizontal one (configured minus true).
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** Documentation and comments only: (a) HANDOFF.md:16: keep the spin result, but say it only shows the horizontal wheel reads sanely. A full turn hides any offset or wheel-diameter error, because that error returns to zero whenever the heading comes back to its start. (b) robot-config.cpp:73-74: repla…
- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** Docs and comment only, no code. (a) HANDOFF.md:16: keep the spin result but say what it shows: the sensors read consistently. It cannot detect wrong offsets or a reversed horizontal wheel, because any constant offset error cancels over a full turn. (b) robot-config.cpp:73-74: replace 'shows up as X…
- **Flags:** severity: low; status: confirmed; fix size: trivial; needs robot: no (small, clearly correct change); changes interface: no; already known: no

ExitCondition starts the timer on the first in-range update and finishes on the next one when time is 0. So largeErrorTimeout = 0 ends every drive 10 ms after it comes within largeError (3 in). Host run: `ExitCondition(3 in, timeout 0) exits on update #2 (10 ms after entering range)`.
- **Suggested fix:** Rewrite both doc lines: say that windupRange 0 means the integral always accumulates and that it resets OUTSIDE the range, and that a range of 0 disables an exit while a timeout of 0 makes it fire almost at once.

### DOCS-14
**slew() limits braking too; 'maximum acceleration' docs and moveToPoint's comment say otherwise**

- **Where:** [src/lemlib/util.cpp:8](../../src/lemlib/util.cpp#L8)
- **What happens:** slew() clamps the change in both directions. Outside the 7.5 in settle zone moveToPoint and moveToPose therefore limit how fast the lateral command can DROP, at 15 per 10 ms with the current config. Going from +127 to 0 takes 9 loops (90 ms, about 5.8 in at about 65 in/s), and to full reverse 17 loops. moveToPoint.cpp:90 says the opposite ('but not for decelerating'). chassis.hpp:75 and robot-config.cpp:103 call the value 'maximum acceleration'. Separately, a negative maxChange sends the output AWAY from the target (slew(100,0,-5) = -5).
- **When:** Tuning: raising or lowering the slew value changes braking authority outside 7.5 in, not just the launch, and the comments lead people to expect otherwise. A negative slew typed into ControllerSettings would make every motion diverge.
- **Evidence:** util.cpp:8-9 `if (change > maxChange) change = maxChange; else if (change < -maxChange) change = -maxChange;`. moveToPoint.cpp:89-91 `// constrain lateral output by max accel // but not for decelerating, since that would interfere with settling` then `if (!close) lateralOut = slew(...)`. Host run: `slew(100,0,-5)=-5 slew(-100,0,-5)=5`.

- **What happens:** With binutils 2.44 every cold and hot link warns 'LOAD segment with RWX permissions', so test_output_2 tags both links [WARNINGS] on every build. Now that LemLib is compiled here, buffer.cpp:9 `task([=]() { taskLoop(); })` also warns that implicit capture of this is deprecated in C++20. A new, real linker or compiler warning looks the same as this noise. The link message also says 'Creating cold package with libc,liblvgl,libm,libpros' and leaves out the two team archives on a fresh build.
- **When:** Any build; in particular a real warning appears after a change and is ignored as the usual noise.
- **Evidence:** Scratch fresh build log: 'Compiled src/lemlib/logger/buffer.cpp [WARNINGS]', 'Creating cold package ... [WARNINGS]', 'Linking hot project ... [WARNINGS]'. Tried in scratch: [=] -> [this] plus `LDFLAGS+=-Wl,--no-warn-rwx-segments` after the include gives 0 WARNINGS and a cold.package.bin `cmp`-identical to the repo's.
- **Suggested fix:** Change [=] to [this] in buffer.cpp:9; verified to leave the cold package byte-identical. Append -Wl,--no-warn-rwx-segments to LDFLAGS in the Makefile, guarded by a $(shell) check that the linker supports it (binutils older than 2.39 would reject it).
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** Same fix as proposed. Two details matter. (1) In src/lemlib/logger/buffer.cpp:9, change `task([=]() { taskLoop(); })` to `task([this]() { taskLoop(); })`. (2) In the Makefile, put the guard AFTER `-include ./common.mk`, because common.mk assigns LDFLAGS with `=` and would throw away an earlier `+=`…
- **Flags:** severity: low; status: confirmed; fix size: trivial; needs robot: no (cannot change behaviour); changes interface: no; already known: no
- **Found by:** build-repo
- **Code check: partly.** The mechanism and the fix both hold up. The claim's wording, "every build prints three", does not. What I checked in the code: - src/lemlib/logger/buffer.cpp:9 is `task([=]() { taskLoop(); }) {}`. common.mk sets `CXX_ST…
- **On-this-robot check: confirmed.** I checked this in a scratch copy of the repo under $TMPDIR and did not touch the repo itself. What the code and toolchain show: - The compiler is Arm GNU Toolchain 14.3.Rel1, and its ld is "GNU ld 2.44.0.20250616". The…

AI : NONE)`, so the 'nothing picked' default 999 becomes QUAL, and a skills default (101 and up) would also become QUAL. ### CLEAN-14
**Overshoot prints as '-0.00' on the brain screen and in the log when there was none**

- **Where:** [src/pidtuner.cpp:288](../../src/pidtuner.cpp#L288)
- **What happens:** minErr starts at 0.0f and result.overshoot = -minErr. When the error never went negative, this is -0.0f, which printf shows as '-0.00' on the [PID] line, the END line and the 'over' metric on the PID tab.
- **When:** Any run that stops short of the target without passing it.
- **Evidence:** In the log: '[PID] TURN ... final=1.29 overshoot=-0.00', 'END run=1 ... over=-0.00' (lines 1636, 1761 and 20 more).
- **Suggested fix:** result.overshoot = std::max(0.0f, -minErr) + 0.0f, or set it only when minErr < 0.
- **Fix as corrected by the code check:** At src/pidtuner.cpp:288 use `result.overshoot = minErr < 0 ? -minErr : 0.0f;` (or `std::max(0.0f, -minErr)`; the extra `+ 0.0f` is not needed). This is one line, has no effect on motion or on the API, and makes the brain output match tools/pidlog.py's own `max(0.0, -min(err))`.
- **Fix as corrected by the on-this-robot check:** At src/pidtuner.cpp:288, write `result.overshoot = std::max(0.0f, -minErr);`. std::max already returns the first argument (+0.0f) when given 0 and -0.0f, so the extra `+ 0.0f` is not needed. `minErr < 0 ? -minErr : 0.0f` works just as well.
- **Flags:** severity: low; status: confirmed; fix size: trivial; needs robot: no (small, clearly correct change); changes interface: no; already known: no

## Disproved and dropped

- **stdout has no lock: printf from two tasks can splice or repeat lines** ([src/pidtuner.cpp:184](../../src/pidtuner.cpp#L184)). Two parts of the claim are true. The rest is not. True: every newlib lock stub is a no-op. Disassembling lib_a-lock.o from firmware/libc.a shows `__retarget_lock_acquire_recursive: bx lr`, and every other lock stub is the same. libpros.a defines only `__env_lock` and `__malloc_lock`. `ser_write_r` in ser_driver.c.o takes and gives `write_mtx` on each call. False: "every task shares stdout's line…
